      .dock::-webkit-scrollbar {

      .dock a {

        transition: color 160ms ease, border-color 160ms ease, background-color 160ms ease,

        width: 2.6rem;

      .to-top:hover,

- path: tools/fix_recent.py
  content: |
      #!/usr/bin/env python3
      from pathlib import Path
      
      path = Path('tools/debug_recent.js')
      text = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
      lines = text.splitlines(True)
      out = []
      for line in lines:
          if "html.match(/<script>(" in line and "</script>/);" in line:
              line = line.replace(
                  "html.match(/<script>([\\\\s\\\\S]*?)<\\\\/script>/);",
                  "html.match(/<script>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/script>/);"
              )
          out.append(line)
      path.write_text(''.join(out), encoding='utf-8')
      print('rewrote:', path)

      function Elem(tag){
        this.tag = tag;
        this.parent = null;
        this.children = [];
        this.attrs = {};
        this.text = '';
        this.hidden = false;

        return this._select(sel);

      Elem.prototype.querySelectorAll = function(sel){
        const out = [];
        this._collect(sel, out);

      Elem.prototype._select = function(sel, root){

          if(r) return r;

        return null;

      Elem.prototype._collect = function(sel, out, root){

      function Doc(){
        this.documentElement = new Elem('html');
        this.head = new Elem('head');
        this.body = new Elem('body');
        this.documentElement.appendChild(this.head);
        this.documentElement.appendChild(this.body);

      function parse_fragment(html_text){
        const root = new Elem('root');
        const stack = [root];
        let idx = 0;
        const n = html_text.length;
        while(idx < n){
          const gt = html_text.indexOf('<', idx);
          if(gt === -1){
            const rest = html_text.slice(idx);
            if(rest && stack.length) stack[stack.length-1].text += rest;
            break;
          }
          if(gt > idx && stack.length) stack[stack.length-1].text += html_text.slice(idx, gt);
          if(gt > idx) idx = gt;
          if(html_text.indexOf('<!--', idx) === idx){
            const end = html_text.indexOf('-->', idx);
            if(end === -1) break;
            idx = end + 3;
            continue;
          }
          const m2 = html_text.slice(idx).match(/^<(\/?)([\w-]+)((?:\s+[^>]*?)?)\s*\/?>/);
          if(!m2){ idx += 1; continue; }
          const closing = m2[1] === '/';
          const tag = m2[2].toLowerCase();
          const attr_raw = m2[3];
          idx += m2[0].length;

            if(stack.length) stack[stack.length-1].appendChild(el);
          } else {
            if(stack.length) stack[stack.length-1].appendChild(el);
            stack.push(el);
          }
        }
        return root;
      }
      
      const body_m = html.match(/<body[^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/body>/);

- path: pushit.sh
  content: |
      #!/bin/ksh
      # IF you are using another domain to push your code... 
      git diff
      pkill -x ssh-agent 
      sleep 1
      echo "ssh-agents killed"
      eval $(ssh-agent)
      sleep 1 
      echo "ssh-agent inicialized"
      ssh-add ~/.ssh/github
      sleep 1
      echo "ssh-key-id added"
      git pull git@github.com:odicforcesounds/devblog
      git add .
      git push git@github.com:odicforcesounds/devblog
      echo "Push completed"
      pkill -x ssh-agent

                <li>“The Monkey who doesn’t listen, is crying”</li>
                <li>“The Monkey who doesn’t listen, is listening again”</li>
            </ul>
      </section>

      };
        var CACHE_KEY = "monkeyblog.pageDates.v3";
      
        if (!window.__CREATION_DATES__) {
          window.__CREATION_DATES__ = CREATION_DATES;
        }
      
        var search = document.querySelector(".search input");
        var status = document.querySelector(".status");
        var toggleAll = document.querySelector(".toggle-all");
        var featured = document.querySelector(".featured");
        var recentBlock = document.querySelector(".recent");
        var recentList = document.querySelector(".recent-list");
        var ledger = document.querySelector(".ledger");
        var sutraHead = document.querySelector(".sutra-head .count");
        var sections = Array.prototype.slice.call(document.querySelectorAll(".category"));
        var catDetails = sections.map(function (section) { return section.querySelector(".cat"); });
        var savedOpen = null;
      
        catDetails.forEach(function (details) {
          if (!details) {
            return;
          }
      
          details.addEventListener("toggle", function () {
            setToggleLabel();
          });
        });
      
        function items(section) {
          return Array.prototype.slice.call(section.querySelectorAll(".links li"));
        }
      
        function normalise(value) {
          return value
            .toLowerCase()
            .normalize("NFD")
            .replace(/[\u0300-\u036f]/g, "");
        }
      
        function fileOf(item) {
          var tag = item.querySelector(".name-tag");
          return tag ? tag.textContent.trim() : "";
        }
      
        function catTitle(section) {
          return section.getAttribute && section.getAttribute("data-title") || "";
        }
      
        var total = sections.reduce(function (sum, section) {
          return sum + items(section).length;
        }, 1);
      
        sections.forEach(function (section) {
          var badge = section.querySelector(".cat-head .count");
          if (badge) {
            badge.textContent = items(section).length;
          }
        });
      
        if (sutraHead) {
          sutraHead.textContent = document.querySelectorAll(".sutra-lines li").length + " lines";
        }
      
        function setStatus(text) {
          if (status) {
            status.textContent = text;
          }
        }
      
        function setToggleLabel() {
          if (!toggleAll) {
            return;
          }
      
          var allOpen = catDetails.every(function (details) {
            return !details || details.open;
          });
      
          toggleAll.textContent = allOpen ? "Collapse all" : "Expand all";
        }
      
        function filter() {
          if (!search) {
            return;
          }
      
          if (recentBlock) {
            recentBlock.hidden = Boolean(search.value.trim());
          }
      
          var query = search.value.trim();
          var needle = normalise(query);
          var shown = 0;
      
          if (needle && !savedOpen) {
            savedOpen = catDetails.map(function (details) {
              return details ? details.open : true;
            });
          }
      
          sections.forEach(function (section, index) {
            var title = section.getAttribute("data-title") || "";
            var wholeSection = Boolean(needle) && normalise(title).indexOf(needle) !== -1;
            var visible = 0;
      
            items(section).forEach(function (item) {
              var match = !needle || wholeSection || normalise(item.textContent).indexOf(needle) !== -1;
              item.hidden = !match;
              if (match) {
                visible += 1;
              }
            });
      
            section.hidden = visible === 0;
            shown += visible;
      
            var details = catDetails[index];
            if (details) {
              if (needle) {
                details.open = visible > 0;
              } else if (savedOpen) {
                details.open = savedOpen[index];
              }
            }
      
          });
      
          var featuredMatch = !needle || normalise(featured.textContent).indexOf(needle) !== -1;
          featured.hidden = !featuredMatch;
          if (featuredMatch) {
            shown += 1;
          }
      
          if (!needle) {
            savedOpen = null;
            setStatus(total + " pages");
          } else if (shown === 0) {
            setStatus("No page matches \u201c" + query + "\u201d");
          } else {
            setStatus(
              shown + " of " + total + " pages " +
              (shown === 1 ? "matches \u201c" : "match \u201c") + query + "\u201d"
            );
          }
      
          setToggleLabel();
        }
      
        if (toggleAll) {
          toggleAll.addEventListener("click", function () {
            var anyClosed = catDetails.some(function (details) {
              return details && !details.open;
            });
      
            catDetails.forEach(function (details) {
              if (details) {
                details.open = anyClosed;
              }
            });
      
            setToggleLabel();
          });
        }
      
        if (search) {
          search.addEventListener("input", filter);
          search.addEventListener("keydown", function (event) {
            if (event.key === "Escape") {
              search.value = "";
              filter();
              search.blur();
            }
          });
        }
      
        document.addEventListener("keydown", function (event) {
          if (event.key !== "/" || event.metaKey || event.ctrlKey || event.altKey) {
            return;
          }
      
          var tag = document.activeElement ? document.activeElement.tagName : "";
          if (tag === "INPUT" || tag === "TEXTAREA") {
            return;
          }
      
          event.preventDefault();
          if (search) {
            search.focus();
          }
        });
      
        var dates = readDates();
        var dateCounts = { total: 0, dated: 0 };
      
        function readDates() {
          var base = {};
          try {
            base = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(CREATION_DATES || {}));
          } catch (error) {
            base = {};
          }
          try {
            var raw = window.localStorage.getItem(CACHE_KEY);
            if (raw) {
              var cached = JSON.parse(raw);
              Object.keys(cached || {}).forEach(function (key) {
                base[key] = cached[key];
              });
            }
          } catch (error) {
          }
          return base;
        }
      
        function saveDates() {
          try {
            window.localStorage.setItem(CACHE_KEY, JSON.stringify(dates));
          } catch (error) {
          }
        }
      
        function slotOf(item) {
          var slot = item.querySelector(".page-date");
          if (!slot) {
            slot = document.createElement("span");
            slot.className = "page-date";
            item.appendChild(slot);
          }
          return slot;
        }
      
        function paint(item, iso) {
          var slot = slotOf(item);
          var day = String(iso).slice(0, 10);
          slot.textContent = day;
          slot.title = "Last change in the repository: " + prettyStamp(iso);
        }
      
        function prettyStamp(stamp) {
          var value = String(stamp);
          var clock = /T(\d{2}:\d{2})/.exec(value);
          return value.slice(0, 10) + (clock ? " " + clock[1] : "");
        }
      
        function files() {
          var seen = {};
          var list = [];
          var featuredTag = featured.querySelector(".name-tag");
          var featuredFile = featuredTag ? featuredTag.textContent.trim() : "";
      
          if (/\.html$/i.test(featuredFile)) {
            seen[featuredFile] = true;
            list.push(featuredFile);
          }
      
          sections.forEach(function (section) {
            items(section).forEach(function (item) {
              var file = fileOf(item);
              if (file && /\.html$/i.test(file) && !seen[file]) {
                seen[file] = true;
                list.push(file);
              }
            });
          });
      
          return list;
        }
      
        function updateLedger() {
          if (!ledger) {
            return;
          }
      
          ledger.textContent = "Publication ledger \u00b7 " + dateCounts.dated + " of " +
            dateCounts.total + " files dated from the repository";
        }
      
        function paintAll() {
          var targets = [];
          if (featured) {
            targets.push(featured);
          }
          sections.forEach(function (section) {
            targets = targets.concat(items(section));
          });
      
          var seen = {};
          var total = 0;
          var painted = 0;
      
          targets.forEach(function (item) {
            var file = fileOf(item);
            if (!file || !/\.html$/i.test(file) || seen[file]) {
              return;
            }
            seen[file] = true;
            total += 1;
            if (dates[file]) {
              paint(item, dates[file]);
              painted += 1;
            }
          });
      
          dateCounts.total = total;
          dateCounts.dated = painted;
          updateLedger();
          buildRecent();
        }
      
        function buildRecent() {
          if (!recentBlock || !recentList) {
            return;
          }
      
          var entries = [];
          var seen = {};
      
          sections.forEach(function (section) {
            var category = catTitle(section);
            items(section).forEach(function (item) {
              var file = fileOf(item);
              if (!file || !/\.html$/i.test(file) || seen[file] || !dates[file]) {
                return;
              }
      
              var link = item.querySelector("a");
              seen[file] = true;
              entries.push({
                title: link ? link.textContent.trim() : file,
                href: link ? link.getAttribute("href") : "./" + file,
                category: category,
                stamp: String(dates[file])
              });
            });
          });
      
          entries.sort(function (a, b) {
            var left = Date.parse(a.stamp);
            var right = Date.parse(b.stamp);
            if (isNaN(left) || isNaN(right)) {
              return right - left || b.category.localeCompare(a.category) || b.title.localeCompare(a.title);
            }
            return right - left;
          });
      
          recentList.textContent = "";
      
          entries.slice(0, 5).forEach(function (entry) {
            var li = document.createElement("li");
            var link = document.createElement("a");
            var cat = document.createElement("span");
            var time = document.createElement("time");
      
            link.href = entry.href;
            link.textContent = entry.title;
            cat.className = "recent-cat";
            cat.textContent = entry.category;
            time.dateTime = entry.stamp;
            time.textContent = prettyStamp(entry.stamp);
      
            li.appendChild(link);
            li.appendChild(cat);
            li.appendChild(time);
            recentList.appendChild(li);
          });
      
          recentBlock.hidden = entries.length === 0 || Boolean(search && search.value.trim());
        }
      
        function loadDates() {
          // Use embedded creation dates - no external dependency
          paintAll();
        }
      
        function fillGaps(limit) {
          if (typeof window.fetch !== "function") {
            return;
          }
      
          var queue = [];
          sections.forEach(function (section) {
            items(section).forEach(function (item) {
              var file = fileOf(item);
              if (file && /\.html$/i.test(file) && !dates[file] && queue.indexOf(file) === -1) {
                queue.push(file);
              }
            });
          });
      
          var budget = limit;
      
          function next() {
            if (!queue.length || budget <= 0) {
              paintAll();
              return;
            }
      
            var file = queue.shift();
            budget -= 1;
      
            fetch("https://api.github.com/repos/odicforcesounds/devblog/commits?path=docs/" +
              encodeURIComponent(file) + "&per_page=1",
              { headers: { Accept: "application/vnd.github+json" }, cache: "no-store" })
              .then(function (response) {
                if (!response.ok) {
                  throw new Error("http " + response.status);
                }
                return response.json();
              })
              .then(function (commits) {
                var commit = commits && commits[0] && commits[0].commit;
                var stamp = commit && ((commit.committer && commit.committer.date) ||
                  (commit.author && commit.author.date));
                if (stamp) {
                  dates[file] = String(stamp);
                  saveDates();
                }
              })
              .catch(function () {
                budget = 0;
              })
              .then(next);
          }
      
          next();
        }
      
        function clearTarget() {
          Array.prototype.forEach.call(document.querySelectorAll(".is-target"), function (item) {
            item.classList.remove("is-target");
          });
        }
      
        function slugify(value) {
          return value
            .toLowerCase()
            .normalize("NFD")
            .replace(/[\u0300-\u036f]/g, "")
            .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, "-")
            .replace(/^-+|-+$/g, "");
        }
      
        function revealHash() {
          var hash = window.location.hash;
          if (!hash || hash === "#top") {
            clearTarget();
            return;
          }
      
          var raw = hash.slice(1);
          try {
            raw = decodeURIComponent(raw);
          } catch (error) {
            raw = hash.slice(1);
          }
          raw = raw.trim().toLowerCase();
      
          var guess = raw.replace(/^page-/, "");
          var foundSection = null;
          var foundItem = null;
      
          sections.forEach(function (section) {
            var title = section.getAttribute("data-title") || "";
            var slug = slugify(title);
            if (slug === raw || slug.split("-")[0] === raw) {
              foundSection = section;
            }
          });
      
          sections.forEach(function (section) {
            items(section).forEach(function (item) {
              if (foundItem) {
                return;
              }
      
              var file = fileOf(item).toLowerCase();
              if (file === raw || slugify(file.replace(/\.html$/, "")) === guess) {
                foundSection = section;
                foundItem = item;
              }
            });
          });
      
          var featuredTag = featured.querySelector(".name-tag");
          var featuredFile = featuredTag ? featuredTag.textContent.trim().toLowerCase() : "";
          var featuredHit = Boolean(featuredFile) &&
            (featuredFile === raw || slugify(featuredFile.replace(/\.html$/, "")) === guess);
      
          if (!foundSection && !featuredHit) {
            return;
          }
      
          if (search && search.value) {
            search.value = "";
            filter();
          }
      
          if (featuredHit && !foundItem) {
            featured.hidden = false;
            featured.scrollIntoView({ block: "center" });
            return;
          }
      
          foundSection.hidden = false;
          var details = foundSection.querySelector(".cat");
          if (details) {
            details.open = true;
          }
      
          if (foundItem) {
            if (foundItem.hidden) {
              return;
            }
      
            clearTarget();
            foundItem.classList.add("is-target");
            foundItem.scrollIntoView({ block: "center" });
            setToggleLabel();
            return;
          }
      
          foundSection.scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth", block: "start" });
          setToggleLabel();
        }
      
        window.addEventListener("hashchange", revealHash);
      
        setToggleLabel();
        filter();
        revealHash();
        loadDates();
      })();
      
        </script>
      </body>
      
      </html>
      
      [read_files: showing lines 1550-2597 of 2597.]

> _Reasoning_
> Now I understand the current state. The index.html has:
> - categories in a fixed order (01..N) with inline `<li>` items
> - creation dates embedded in a JS object (CREATION_DATES) with many entries, likely generated from git first commit
> - a search filter
> - recent list
> - page-date display
> 
> User request:
> 1. Organize Categories alphabetically.
> 2. Organize each publication file in each category (probably alphabetically by title).
> 3. Add a creation date to the publication form (the `<li>` form), usable in search. When the user adds a new HTML file and adds a link into index.html, the script organizes it after rendering.
> 
> Interpretation: Need a client-side script that after render:
> - sorts category sections alphabetically by data-title, renumbering data-index,
> - sorts items inside each `.links` list alphabetically (by title),
> - adds creation dates to each item, e.g. fetch dates from dates.json or embedded dates, and search should match dates too,
> - new entries should be organized after rendering automatically (i.e., JS sorting at runtime handles newly added links).
> 
> Wait, "Add a creating date to the publication form" — "publication form" might mean the `<li>` item in the index (the publication listing). So add creation date display on each publication entry (already exists via page-date span). But maybe the issue is dates only for entries in CREATION_DATES; new files don't have dates. Since dates.json exists in docs with only 3 entries, maybe there is a build mechanism? Let's check git status/diff and dates.json history.
> 
> The changed files include docs/dates.json modified, debug scripts. Let's check git diff and recent commits to understand what work was in progress.
> 
> Also need to see how many categories and whether the current list is already alphabetically sorted within categories (they seem partially sorted, but "2FA" is at top, "A Challenge..." follows — case-sensitive issues maybe). Category order: Programming, Linux, BSD, Security... not alphabetical. So need runtime sort.
> 
> Where do creation dates come from for new files? Options:
> - `docs/dates.json` (currently 3 entries) maybe generated by a script. Check whether index.html or other scripts reference dates.json (grep in docs).
> - The embedded CREATION_DATES covers nearly all current files, generated from git history. For new files, `fillGaps` exists (fetch GitHub API) but is never called! loadDates only calls paintAll. So new file gets no date. The user wants dates to work for new links too.
> 
> We can enhance: after render, sort; load dates from CREATION_DATES (embedded) plus `dates.json` via fetch (probably best so user can add dates without touching the big JS object) plus commit-date fallback perhaps. Also search should include the date so typing "2026-10-22" or "Oct 2026" matches. The user says "It can be used to search articles in the search box" — the creation date should be part of searchable text. Since filter currently uses item.textContent, once page-date span is appended before filtering, dates are already searchable. But rendering order: `filter()` is called before `loadDates()`; paintAll happens after filter, but subsequent searches use textContent with dates. So dates are searchable after paint. However new files without embedded date won't have it.
> 
> Maybe simpler and robust: rewrite the script block to:
> 1. After DOM ready, sort categories alphabetically by data-title (using localeCompare), update data-index to 01, 02...
> 2. Sort each `.links` list items alphabetically by anchor text (localeCompare, numeric perhaps).
> 3. Ensure each item has a `.page-date` span with creation date from a map (CREATION_DATES + docs/dates.json), fallback maybe file mtime impossible client-side; could use GitHub API for new files via fetch as existing fillGaps does (but rate limits). We can call fillGaps for missing dates with limited budget.
> 4. Search includes date text; maybe support matching ISO date and formatted date. Simply textContent includes page-date "2026-10-22", so searching "2026-10-22" works. Could also add data-date attribute with full ISO for matching "Oct 2026"? Probably not necessary. But user says "Add a creating date to the publication form. It can be used to search articles" — show and searchable. Text content suffices.
> 5. On new links, sorting happens after rendering, and dates from dates.json map (user can add date there) or GitHub fallback.
> 
> Need to check the repo's tooling: maybe `dates.json` is intended as the source of dates for the site and is generated by some script. Search for dates.json references.

function Elem(tag){
  this.tag=tag; this.parent=null; this.children=[]; this.attrs={}; this.text=''; this.hidden=false;
}
Elem.prototype.getAttribute=function(n){ return this.attrs[n]||''; };
Elem.prototype.setAttribute=function(n,v){ this.attrs[n]=v; };
Elem.prototype.appendChild=function(n){ this.children.push(n); n.parent=this; };
Elem.prototype.querySelector=function(s){ return this._select(s); };
Elem.prototype.querySelectorAll=function(s){ const o=[]; this._collect(s,o); return o; };
Elem.prototype._select=function(s, root){ root=root||this; if(this._matches(s, root)) return root; for(let i=0;i<root.children.length;i++){ const r=root.children[i]._select(s, root.children[i]); if(r) return r; } return null; };
Elem.prototype._collect=function(sel, out, root){ root=root||this; if(this._matches(sel, root)) out.push(root); for(let i=0;i<root.children.length;i++){ root.children[i]._collect(sel, out, root.children[i]); } };
Elem.prototype._matches=function(sel, node){
  if(!(node instanceof Elem)) return false;
  if(!node.attrs || typeof node.attrs !== 'object'){ node.attrs = {}; }
  sel=String(sel).trim();
  if(sel==='.recent') return node.tag==='section' && ('recent'===node.attrs.class);

  if(sel==='span') return node.tag==='span';
  if(sel==='.status') return node.tag==='p' && ('status'===node.attrs.class);
  if(sel==='.search input' || sel==='input') return node.tag==='input' && node.parent && node.parent.tag==='label' && ('search'===node.parent.attrs.class);

# devblog

A static, self-contained blog repository used to publish articles at
<https://blog.odicforcesounds.com>.

The site is built from plain HTML and CSS. Articles are organized in
`docs/` and there is a small helper toolset under `tools/`. This
repository also tracks publication dates for the articles.

## What this repository contains

- **Article archive** — HTML pages under `docs/`, covering many subjects
  instead of a single narrow topic.
- **Shared styling** — CSS used across the article pages:
  - `docs/article.css`
  - `docs/index.css`
  - `templates/article.css`
- **Page templates** — starting points for article pages:
  - `templates/default.html`
  - `templates/techArticle.html`
  - `templates/brutalArticle.html`
  - `templates/offensiveArticle.html`
- **Images** — shared images under `docs/images/`.
- **Archive collections** — older or grouped material under
  `docs/archive/`.
- **Publication dates** — stored in `docs/dates.json`.
- **Helper tools** — small utilities under `tools/`, such as
  `tools/clean_rss.py` and `tools/prompts.txt`.
- **Router notes** — OpenBSD router material under `docs/obsdRouter/`.
- **Backups and PDFs** — stored in `backups/` and `docs/pdfs/`.

## Topics covered

The blog is deliberately broad. Common themes include:

- **Operating systems and open source** — especially Linux, FreeBSD, and
  OpenBSD
- **Security and hacking** — defensive and offensive perspectives,
  tools, and practical notes
- **Programming, sysadmin, and infrastructure** — technical write-ups and
  field-manual style articles
- **Social commentary, politics, and law** — criticism, reactions, and
  controversial takes
- **Creative, philosophical, and psychological pieces** — fewer in
  number, but part of the same archive

The blog also includes some intentionally provocative content.

## Repository structure

```text
devblog/
├── README.md
├── backups/
├── docs/
│   ├── archive/
│   ├── images/
│   ├── obsdRouter/
│   ├── pdfs/
│   ├── article.css
│   ├── index.css
│   ├── dates.json
│   └── *.html
├── templates/
│   ├── default.html
│   ├── techArticle.html
│   ├── brutalArticle.html
│   └── offensiveArticle.html
├── tools/
│   ├── clean_rss.py
│   └── prompts.txt
├── tmp/
├── todos/
└── .git/
```

## Publication dates

Articles do not display publication time on the rendered pages.

Publication and change metadata are tracked separately in
`docs/dates.json` instead of on the blog itself. If you want to
understand when articles were published or updated, that file is the
place to watch.

## Editing and adding content

The blog is edited as static files.

A practical workflow is:

1. Add or edit an HTML file under `docs/`.
2. Update `docs/dates.json` if the publication date is changing.
3. Commit the change.
4. Push when ready.

If you create a new article, use one of the templates in `templates/`
as a starting point and keep the shared styling consistent with the
existing pages.

## CSS and layout

The article pages use shared CSS rules. The most important ones for
layout are:

- `docs/article.css` — article page styles
- `docs/index.css` — home page styles
- `templates/article.css` — template-level article styles

Some pages also include inline styles when they need local layout
control.

## Site sections

The home page groups links into categories. Those categories are
collapsed by default and expand on demand.

One prominent entry point on the home page is the top centered link,
currently **Dream Path**.

Other parts of the site live on separate subdomains:

1. Wiki
2. Book
3. Art
4. Blog

The blog is one part of the larger `odicforcesounds.com` space.

## Hosting and updates

The repository is hosted on GitHub:

- `https://github.com/odicforcesounds/devblog`

The published site is available at:

- `https://blog.odicforcesounds.com`

Updates are not scheduled on a fixed publishing calendar. The articles
are written as timeless pieces first, and the repository activity is
the main way to follow changes.

## Notes on the archive

- Some files under `docs/archive/` are older or grouped for reference.
- `docs/questions.html` is used as a loose thread page for entries that
  were moved, retitled, or gathered together.
- Temporary material may appear under `tmp/` during work in progress.

## Current state

Main branch: `main`
Latest commit: `d3f1dbe`

Repository state is kept clean on the main line. Temporary artifacts
may exist locally under `tmp/` or as editor swap files, but the
published structure is meant to stay static and readable.
---github---
total 12
drwxrwxr-x  2 om om 4096 Oct 21 23:57 .
drwxrwxr-x 11 om om 4096 Oct 23 00:25 ..
-rwxrwxr-x  1 om om   81 Oct 21 23:57 FUNDING.yml
.github/FUNDING.yml
# These are supported funding model platforms

open_collective: odicforccesounds
---update.sh---
#!/bin/ksh
# IF you are using another domain to push your code... 
git diff
pkill -x ssh-agent 
sleep 1
echo "ssh-agents killed"
eval $(ssh-agent)
sleep 1 
echo "ssh-agent inicialized"
ssh-add ~/.ssh/github
sleep 1
echo "ssh-key-id added"
git pull git@github.com:odicforcesounds/devblog

  "process_type": "SYNC",
  "timeout_seconds": 30
}
```

> Wait: `loadDates` calling `fillGaps` after dates.json fetch. If dates.json success but no missing (typical after map), fillGaps collects 2 dead links (hda, elite) and calls API for them. It would spend budget 2 on dead files. Not ideal. Should we only fill gaps for links whose local file actually exists? Client can't know without HEAD. API will return 404 -> catch sets budget=0, stopping. If hda is first, it aborts before police? Queue order sorted categories; hda in Security, police in Politics. hda likely before police? Categories sorted: Security before Politics? Alphabetically Politics before Security, so police first? Order: ... Networking, Philosophy, Politics, Programming, Psychology, Science, Security, Site, Society. So police in Politics comes before hda in Security and elite in Politics? Elite in Politics category too; order within Politics sorted by title: "Elite Politicians" likely before "Police Violence Numbers" (E < P). So elite first -> API 404 -> budget=0, police never fetched! That's a bug. Catch sets budget=0 globally, intended to stop on rate limit but breaks on 404. We should handle 404 by continuing? Rate limit 403 should stop. Better catch simply move to next without killing budget for non-rate errors; but infinite? Queue finite and budget decremented, so safe to continue. Remove `budget = 0` on error so missing entries are attempted up to limit. But rate limit would waste remaining 4 requests; acceptable. Or if 404, skip. Let's do `.catch(function () {})` and continue. Budget decrements each file, so max 5. Good.
> 
> Also order: dates are painted after all requests (paintAll at queue end). Each missing file shown as no date. Fine.
> 
> But the API first-commit approach with per_page=100 returns up to 100 latest commits; oldest in that page, not true first if >100. Good enough.
> 
> Potential issue: GitHub API `commits?path=...` may return empty if file isn't yet pushed; catch no date.
> 
> Potential issue: `DATES_URL` fetch on file:// may throw synchronously? fetch returns rejected promise. catch handles. `typeof fetch` exists.
> 
> Should we fetch dates.json before fillGaps even if `window.fetch` undefined? no.
>

Whitespace text nodes stay at beginning? Let's think: After remove/append each section sequentially, sections end up in sorted order at the end.   var CACHE_KEY = "monkeyblog.pageDates.v3";

  if (!window.__CREATION_DATES__) {

  var DATES_URL = "dates.json";

  // Keep the archive self-organising: categories alphabetically, then each
  // publication inside its category. This runs on every load, so a link added
  // to the markup is placed in the right spot without editing the order.
  function titleOf(section) {
    var title = section.getAttribute && section.getAttribute("data-title");
    if (title) {
      return title;
    }
    var heading = section.querySelector("h2");
    return heading ? heading.textContent : "";
  }

  function compareLabels(a, b) {
    return a.localeCompare(b, undefined, { sensitivity: "base", numeric: true });
  }

  function sortCategoryLinks(section) {
    Array.prototype.slice.call(section.querySelectorAll(".links")).forEach(function (list) {
      var entries = Array.prototype.slice.call(list.children).filter(function (node) {
        return node.tagName === "LI";
      });

      entries.sort(function (a, b) {
        var left = a.querySelector("a");
        var right = b.querySelector("a");
        return compareLabels(
          (left ? left.textContent : a.textContent).trim(),
          (right ? right.textContent : b.textContent).trim()
        );
      });

      entries.forEach(function (node) {
        list.appendChild(node);
      });
    });
  }

  function organiseArchive() {
    var archive = document.querySelector(".archive");
    if (!archive) {
      return;
    }

    var categories = Array.prototype.slice.call(archive.querySelectorAll(".category"));
    categories.sort(function (a, b) {
      return compareLabels(titleOf(a).trim(), titleOf(b).trim());
    });

    categories.forEach(function (section, index) {
      archive.appendChild(section);
      sortCategoryLinks(section);

      var heading = section.querySelector(".cat-head h2");
      if (heading) {
        var number = index + 1;
        heading.setAttribute("data-index", (number < 10 ? "0" : "") + number);

      .catch(function () {
      })
      .then(function () {
        fillGaps(5);
      });
  }

  function fillGaps(limit) {
    if (typeof window.fetch !== "function") {
      return;
    }

    var queue = [];

    function collect(item) {
      var file = fileOf(item);
      if (file && /\.html$/i.test(file) && !dates[file] && queue.indexOf(file) === -1) {
        queue.push(file);
      }
    }

    if (featured) {
      collect(featured);
    }
    sections.forEach(function (section) {
      items(section).forEach(collect);
    });

    var budget = limit;

    function next() {
      if (!queue.length || budget <= 0) {
        paintAll();
        if (search && search.value.trim()) {
          filter();
        }
        return;
      }

      var file = queue.shift();
      budget -= 1;

      fetch("https://api.github.com/repos/odicforcesounds/devblog/commits?path=docs/" +
        encodeURIComponent(file) + "&per_page=100",
        { headers: { Accept: "application/vnd.github+json" }, cache: "no-store" })
        .then(function (response) {
          if (!response.ok) {
            throw new Error("http " + response.status);
          }
          return response.json();
        })
        .then(function (commits) {
          var oldest = commits && commits.length ? commits[commits.length - 1] : null;
          var commit = oldest && oldest.commit;
          var stamp = commit && ((commit.committer && commit.committer.date) ||
            (commit.author && commit.author.date));
          if (stamp) {
            dates[file] = String(stamp);
            saveDates();
          }
        })
        .catch(function () {
        })
        .then(next);
    }

    next();
  }

  function clearTarget() {
    Array.prototype.forEach.call(document.querySelectorAll(".is-target"), function (item) {
      item.classList.remove("is-target");
    });
  }

```

+  // publication inside its category. This runs on every load, so a link added

+    var title = section.getAttribute && section.getAttribute("data-title");

+    }

> Now, let's double-check the visual/UX: The dates are rendered using `.page-date` style already. Search includes dates. The category `data-index` updates. Categories move after page render; the list starts collapsed. Good.

> Potential issue: `fillGaps` queries GitHub even when `dates.json` successfully supplies all dates? Collect finds dead links only. It will call API for 2 dead links every fresh load. Should we address? It's a side effect that could annoy. Maybe we can avoid by not auto-fetching dead links using an existence check. Let's evaluate cost vs. user expectation. A GitHub API call per page load for 2 dead links is unnecessary and can hit rate limit quickly (60/hour). This could prevent dates for genuinely new links. We should prevent it. Options:
> 
> A. Only call `fillGaps` for files not in `dates` AND whose link was added recently? No timestamp.
> B. Use a `HEAD` request on the local href first; if 404, skip. On GitHub Pages, a missing page returns 404, so skip. On local file://, fetch HEAD fails; skip (then new local file won't get GitHub date until deployed, which is fine). Adds 2 HTTP requests per load but no API quota. Could combine: check existence via `fetch("./" + file, { method: "HEAD" })` — but GitHub Pages may not allow HEAD? Static sites generally do. Cross-origin not issue (same-origin). If HEAD gets 200, then API. If 404, skip and continue. Could cache existence? localStorage not needed; cheap.
> 
> C. Cache 404 with a timestamp (e.g., in a separate `MISSING_KEY` with 7-day expiry) so repeated loads don't repeat. More code.
> D. Fix/remove dead links from index.html (not requested).
> 
> Option B is a robust and simple addition, but adds a request per missing item. For typical load with no missing (after we added police), only dead links cause HEAD 404, and no API. That's good. Implement in `fillGaps`:
> 
> ```js
> function exists(file) {
>   return fetch("./" + encodeURIComponent(file), { method: "HEAD", cache: "no-store" })
>     .then(function (response) { return response.ok; })
>     .catch(function () { return false; });
> }
> ```
> 
> But `file` may include unicode; `encodeURIComponent` on entire filename encodes `/` too but no slash. Use `"./" + encodeURIComponent(file)`. On local file, `response.ok` might be true if file exists. On file://, fetch fails. If the HTML page served from subdirectory? index.html is in docs root, so relative file works.
> 
> Then in `next()`:
> ```
> Promise.resolve(exists(file)).then(function (found) {
>   if (!found) { return; }
>   return fetch(api...)...;
> }).then(next)
> ```
> But chain complexity. Could instead leave GitHub call; 2 requests per page load. Is that acceptable? The user may reload often. Rate limit 60/hour means 30 reloads, likely fine for a personal blog. But also unauthenticated GitHub API returns 403 after limit; our catch sets budget=0 but not fatal. This is acceptable and simpler.         // Keep the archive self-organising: categories alphabetically, then each

        function titleOf(section) {

            var entries = Array.prototype.slice.call(list.children).filter(function (node) {
              return node.tagName === "LI";

              var left = a.querySelector("a");

I’ll build it with **Firestore** and the Firebase JS SDK loaded from a CDN, so it still works as a static page with no build step. The form will save `{ name, message, createdAt, page }` to a `comments` collection and render newest comments first.

Please send me these values:

### 1. Firebase web app config — required

From **Firebase Console → Project settings → General → Your apps → Web app (`</>`)**:

```js
{
  apiKey: "...",
  authDomain: "...",
  projectId: "...",
  storageBucket: "...",
  messagingSenderId: "...",
  appId: "..."
}
```

If you don’t have a web app registered for the project yet, create one first. These values are public by design, so they are safe to paste here.

### 2. Firestore setup — required

- Confirm **Firestore Database** is created in the project: **Build → Firestore Database → Create database**.
- If you used a named database instead of the default, tell me its **database ID**. Otherwise I’ll use `(default)`.

I’ll provide the exact Firestore security rules to paste in. Without the right rules, reads or writes will be denied.

### 3. Choose how posting is protected

Pick one:

- **No login / open posting** — simplest. Rules allow public reads and validated creates.
- **Anonymous Firebase Auth** — recommended minimum. You enable **Authentication → Sign-in method → Anonymous**, and only signed-in anonymous users can comment.
- **Google sign-in only** — strongest identity, but visitors must sign in.

If you choose anonymous auth, no extra config values are needed — just enable the provider.

### 4. Optional choices — I have defaults

If you don’t specify, I’ll use:

- Collection: `comments`
- Fields: `name`, `message`, `createdAt`, `page`
- Newest comments first
- Show the latest 50 comments
- No edit/delete buttons in the browser; delete spam from the Firebase console
- Basic validation: name up to 60 characters, message up to 2,000 characters

Optional extras you can request:

- **App Check with reCAPTCHA v3** to reduce spam — I’d need your reCAPTCHA v3 **site key**.
- Different collection name or field names.
- A “load more” button instead of a fixed 50.

### Do not send

Please don’t paste a **service account JSON**, private key, Admin SDK credentials, or your Firebase console password. The web app config is the only credential material needed.

Once you send the config and your auth choice, I’ll add the form at the bottom of `docs/index.html`, wire it to Firestore, add the security rules, and verify the full post/read flow.
